Abstract NLD-Uni is a unified discrete diffusion language model that combines visual understanding and generation via cross-paradigm on-policy distillation and progressive self-refining diffusion.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small We propose Nemotron-Labs-Diffusion-Uni (NLD-Uni), a unified discrete diffusion large language model (DLLM) for visual understanding and generation tasks. Compared with prior unified diffusion models, NLD-Uni introduces two major innovations. First, we propose a unified cross-paradigm on-policy distillation (OPD) framework that successfully transfers the visual understanding ability of autoregressive VLMs and visual generation ability of continuous flow models, to a unified DLLM. Second, NLD-Uni incorporates a novel progressive self-refining diffusion process that gradually corrupts each token across multiple steps during training, as opposed to adopting the conventional masked diffusion formulation that directly corrupts a clean image token to an uninformative mask token. At inference, this design allows NLD-Uni to make intermediate commit to a token, enabling progressive self-refinement for better visual quality. Through extensive experiments, we show that NLD-Uni achieves state-of-the-art performance among unified DLLMs on diverse tasks including visual question-answering, multi-modal reasoning, object grounding, text-to-image generation, and image editing.

[28.10.2026 06:41] Claude request. Model: claude-haiku-5-5. Prompt: Read an abstract of the ML paper and return a JSON with fields: 'desc': explanation of the paper in Russian (4 sentences), use correct machine learning terms. Abstract SPOT accelerates diffusion language models by predicting token generation order and verifying candidates in parallel, reducing passes up to 6.8x while maintaining exact decoding.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Diffusion language models (DLMs) promise parallel text generation, but in practice face an unresolved speed-quality trade-off. Exact decoding fills in only one masked position per model pass, while faster multi-token decoding schemes are lossy. Speculative decoding is the standard lossless acceleration for autoregressive models. However, it does not transfer directly to DLMs, which must choose both what to generate and where. We show that this order is the missing piece: the model's own predictions from the previous pass already give the right token at over 90% of positions, but not which position comes next. We introduce Speculating Positions Order Trajectory (SPOT), a 0.62M-parameter model that reads only the target's output distributions, predicts this order and proposes a tree of candidate continuations, which the target verifies in a single pass by state equality. Coupled-noise match, which shares Gumbel noise between drafter and target, keeps this verification exact at any temperature. This approach also inspired us to introduce SPOT-Sets for dynamic threshold decoding, where a pass commits several positions: SPOT-Sets drafts whole commit sets together with their tokens. Our comprehensive experiments show that SPOT cuts target passes by up to 6.8times and reaches up to 3.4times the throughput of standard decoding on SDAR-8B, matching or beating the calibrated and training-free drafting baselines in every task and budget at T=0 and T=1. In addition, we show that SPOT's learned drafter transfers across models, demonstrating successful transfer across six different DLMs; with a light calibration, the SPOT-Sets drafter transfers as well. On top of dynamic threshold decoding, SPOT-Sets improves the throughput of SDAR-8B by 59% on average over the threshold decoder alone. Project page: https://eliezer318.github.io/SPOT-dLLM/

Paper text to classify:

"Abstract SPOT accelerates diffusion language models by predicting token generation order and verifying candidates in parallel, reducing passes up to 6.8x while maintaining exact decoding.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Diffusion language models (DLMs) promise parallel text generation, but in practice face an unresolved speed-quality trade-off. Exact decoding fills in only one masked position per model pass, while faster multi-token decoding schemes are lossy. Speculative decoding is the standard lossless acceleration for autoregressive models. However, it does not transfer directly to DLMs, which must choose both what to generate and where. We show that this order is the missing piece: the model's own predictions from the previous pass already give the right token at over 90% of positions, but not which position comes next. We introduce Speculating Positions Order Trajectory (SPOT), a 0.62M-parameter model that reads only the target's output distributions, predicts this order and proposes a tree of candidate continuations, which the target verifies in a single pass by state equality. Coupled-noise match, which shares Gumbel noise between drafter and target, keeps this verification exact at any temperature. This approach also inspired us to introduce SPOT-Sets for dynamic threshold decoding, where a pass commits several positions: SPOT-Sets drafts whole commit sets together with their tokens. Our comprehensive experiments show that SPOT cuts target passes by up to 6.8times and reaches up to 3.4times the throughput of standard decoding on SDAR-8B, matching or beating the calibrated and training-free drafting baselines in every task and budget at T=0 and T=1. In addition, we show that SPOT's learned drafter transfers across models, demonstrating successful transfer across six different DLMs; with a light calibration, the SPOT-Sets drafter transfers as well. On top of dynamic threshold decoding, SPOT-Sets improves the throughput of SDAR-8B by 59% on average over the threshold decoder alone. Project page: https://eliezer318.github.io/SPOT-dLLM/"

Abstract A world model predicts machine learning program outcomes to enable affordable test-time scaling for AI research agents, improving performance with fewer real executions.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small AI research agents can propose, execute, and improve programs that solve machine learning tasks. However, executing each candidate program requires training and evaluating ML models, limiting how many candidates an agent can explore at each step. We introduce MLE-World, a world model finetuned from Qwen3.5-4B that predicts a program's terminal output (error traces, training logs, and validation scores) from its code and data description without executing it, together with a confidence score estimating the prediction's correctness. Because a prediction costs one inference call rather than a training run, MLE-World makes test time scaling (TTS) affordable for these agents. We build five TTS policies, guided by MLE-World, that compare several candidates per step by their predicted outputs: Sequential revision, Best-of-N, Pairwise judging, Fusion, and a UCB style rule that also adds an exploration bonus from this confidence score. Since predictions can be wrong, we run a candidate for real only when its confidence score is low. On 17 MLE-bench Lite tasks, MLE-World matches the real terminal output on 77.9% of held out programs, versus 41.1% for much larger prompted models. MLE-World guided TTS achieves up to 22.1% higher observed mean performance than the agent without a world model, with less real execution. Project webpage: https://mle-world.github.io/.
[28.10.2026 06:41] Response: {"desc": "Статья представляет MLE-World — world model на основе Qwen3.5-4B, которая предсказывает результат выполнения ML-программы (логи ошибок, данные обучения и валидационные метрики) по коду и описанию данных, не запуская её. Модель также выдаёт confidence score, оценивающий надёжность предсказания. Благодаря тому, что предсказание требует лишь одного вызова LLM вместо обучения модели, test-time scaling становится дешёвым для AI-агентов, которые исследуют кандидатов на решение задач машинного обучения. На 17 задачах MLE-bench Lite MLE-World точно воспроизводит реальный результат в 77.9% случаев, а агент с её помощью достигает до 22.1% более высокой средней производительности при меньшем числе реальных запусков.", "emoji": "🌍", "title": "Предсказывай результат ML-программы, не запуская её"}

Abstract Timestral is a family of time series foundation models that achieves state-of-the-art probabilistic forecasting through decoupled horizon forecasting, native future covariate support, and synthetic pretraining followed by mixed post-training.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small We introduce Timestral, a family of four time series foundation models for probabilistic forecasting, with 5M, 28M, 123M, and 209M parameters. Timestral sets a new state of the art on GIFT-Eval, TIME, and fev-bench, where Timestral-209M and Timestral-123M take the top two places on every metric. We achieve this with three ingredients: Decoupled Horizon Forecasting (DHF), which separates prediction tokens from the causally encoded context, so that longer horizons are trained without masking context and each observed value is both a forecast target and context for later forecasts; a covariate framework that natively supports known-future covariates within a causal architecture; and a two-stage pipeline that pretrains exclusively on a diverse, large-scale synthetic corpus before post-training on a mix of real and synthetic data with extended context. This report describes the model design and its benchmark results. Weights for all four models are available on Hugging Face and inference code on GitHub.
[28.10.2026 06:41] Response: {"desc": "В статье представлено семейство моделей-фундаментов для временных рядов Timestral, состоящее из четырёх моделей от 5M до 209M параметров. Ключевой метод — Decoupled Horizon Forecasting (DHF), который отделяет токены предсказания от каузально закодированного контекста, позволяя обучать длинные горизонты без маскирования контекста. Модели поддерживают известные будущие ковариаты внутри каузальной архитектуры и обучаются в два этапа: предобучение только на большом синтетическом корпусе и дообучение на смеси реальных и синтетических данных с расширенным контекстом. "Abstract Timestral is a family of time series foundation models that achieves state-of-the-art probabilistic forecasting through decoupled horizon forecasting, native future covariate support, and synthetic pretraining followed by mixed post-training.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small We introduce Timestral, a family of four time series foundation models for probabilistic forecasting, with 5M, 28M, 123M, and 209M parameters. Timestral sets a new state of the art on GIFT-Eval, TIME, and fev-bench, where Timestral-209M and Timestral-123M take the top two places on every metric. We achieve this with three ingredients: Decoupled Horizon Forecasting (DHF), which separates prediction tokens from the causally encoded context, so that longer horizons are trained without masking context and each observed value is both a forecast target and context for later forecasts; a covariate framework that natively supports known-future covariates within a causal architecture; and a two-stage pipeline that pretrains exclusively on a diverse, large-scale synthetic corpus before post-training on a mix of real and synthetic data with extended context. This report describes the model design and its benchmark results. Weights for all four models are available on Hugging Face and inference code on GitHub."

Abstract Physevolve trains vision-language models in causal physical reasoning by using a physics engine to validate self-generated executable worlds and counterfactual simulations, improving performance on dynamics benchmarks without human data.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Physical reasoning underpins how embodied systems understand and act in the physical world, yet vision-language models (VLMs) remain unreliable on questions about dynamics and physical causality. Progress is critically constrained by the scarcity of grounded counterfactual training data, as observational videos capture only the factual trajectory that occurred. The self-evolving paradigm offers a route beyond fixed datasets by enabling models to construct their own training problems. Extending this paradigm to physics, however, requires an external source of truth that can both validate model-proposed environments and verify alternative physical outcomes under intervention. We introduce , a verifier-grounded self-evolving framework that trains VLMs for causal physical reasoning from model-proposed executable worlds and simulation-authored tasks. At each round, a shared VLM proposes executable world programs and evidence schemas; a deterministic physics engine freezes each valid world, simulates matched baseline and body-elided counterfactual branches, and synthesizes discrete, answer-bound visual tasks. Exact verifier feedback from task solving updates both the shared policy via GRPO and an adaptive curriculum over physical intents. Starting from Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct,  achieves targeted gains across PhysBench, CausalPhys, and ContPhy without human intervention data, improving PhysBench Throwing by 8.8 points and ContPhy Counterfactual Removal and Goal-Pit by 7.4 and 24.4 points. These gains concentrate on interaction-sensitive and altered-world questions, demonstrating zero-shot transfer of causal principles to unseen complex dynamics (cloth, fluid, soft bodies) and real-world video.

Модель сама предлагает исполняемые программы миров, а детерминированный физический движок проверяет их и генерирует контрфактические сценарии, например, с удалённым телом. Точная обратная связь от верификатора обновляет политику через GRPO и адаптивный учебный план по физическим интентам. Abstract MoveBench introduces a specialized benchmark for evaluating large language models on sports and health coaching, emphasizing safety, scientific validity, and personalization across multiple-choice and open-ended tasks.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Despite rapid advances in large language models (LLMs), their deployment in the sports and health domain remains high-risk due to the absence of domain-specific evaluation benchmarks. Existing general-purpose benchmarks (e.g., MMLU and HELM) primarily assess broad knowledge and reasoning capabilities, but fail to capture critical domain requirements such as exercise safety, physiological appropriateness, personalization, and actionable coaching quality. This gap is particularly concerning in fitness and health applications, where incorrect or unsafe recommendations can lead to physical harm. To address this limitation, we introduce MoveBench, the first comprehensive benchmarking framework specifically designed for evaluating LLMs in sports and health coaching. The proposed benchmark integrates heterogeneous task formats, including multiple-choice questions (MCQs) and open-ended question answering (OpenQA), grounded in real-world user scenarios and paired with structured rubrics emphasizing safety compliance, scientific validity, and training logic. Through large-scale evaluation of 15 diverse LLMs on 809 questions with 5,012 rubric-level criteria, our results reveal substantial performance variability, particularly in safety awareness, structured program design, and personalization capability, highlighting a critical gap between knowing and doing. Our work establishes a foundational evaluation standard for sports and health LLM applications and provides a reproducible framework for advancing safe, reliable, and context-aware AI-driven coaching systems. Open resources are available at https://github.com/SatoSakula/MoveBench.

Существующие общие тесты вроде MMLU и HELM измеряют широкие знания и рассуждения, но не учитывают безопасность упражнений, физиологическую уместность и персонализацию. Our work establishes a foundational evaluation standard for sports and health LLM applications and provides a reproducible framework for advancing safe, reliable, and context-aware AI-driven coaching systems. Open resources are available at https://github.com/SatoSakula/MoveBench."

Prompt: Read an abstract of the ML paper and return a JSON with fields: 'desc': explanation of the paper in Russian (4 sentences), use correct machine learning terms. Abstract GeoUG improves spatial reasoning by combining depth and multi-view generation with adaptive geometric verification during inference.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Image and video generators can produce spatially consistent visual content, but how to use this capability to improve spatial understanding remains underexplored. We study this problem from two perspectives: geometric supervision during training and explicit geometric evidence during inference. To this end, we introduce a unified geometric understanding and generation model (GeoUG) that integrates depth and multi-view generation with spatial understanding. First, we design a multi-scale depth codec that enables metric-depth generation using a pretrained visual autoencoder, and show that joint training yields task-dependent gains in spatial understanding. Second, we introduce 3DCueBench to control geometric difficulty while balancing selected 2D cues, and find that generated depth provides larger reasoning gains on questions requiring fine-grained geometric discrimination. Building on this finding, we develop adaptive interleaved reasoning, in which GeoUG first drafts an answer and then decides whether to generate geometric evidence to confirm or revise it. We train this decision through reinforcement learning, balancing accuracy gains over retaining the draft against generation cost. GeoUG achieves circular accuracies of 97.9%, 90.8%, and 94.2% on CV-Bench-3D, 3DCueBench, and DA-2K with fewer generation calls than always generating.

Авторы вводят multi-scale depth codec на основе предобученного visual autoencoder для генерации metric depth и показывают, что совместное обучение улучшает spatial understanding. Для контроля сложности предложен бенчмарк 3DCueBench, на котором генерируемая глубина дает наибольший прирост в задачах тонкого геометрического различения. Модель использует adaptive interleaved reasoning: сначала строит черновой ответ, затем через reinforcement learning решает, стоит ли генерировать геометрические доказательства, балансируя точность и стоимость генерации.", "emoji": "🧭", "title": "Сначала черновик, затем геометрическая проверка: генерация глубины помогает ИИ лучше понимать пространство"}
